I also noticed one of my books, that P had been reading in my room, was gone.</p><p><br></p><p>Another friend, who was at P's house said he'd seen it there and P was showing it to him. I was furious and felt betrayed.  I demanded the book back and he denied it. The other friend stole it back for me (it was mine and had my name in it) when he visited P's house.</p><p><br></p><p>Here's where I feel I went OTT. This went on for four or 5 weeks and included my friend group. I think this went too far.  I was angry and upset at the time and felt he needed to be punished.  As a man, I see P's behaviour as the actions of of a socially awkward and immature boy and my retaliation, especially with the power dynamic, was way too far.</p><p><br></p><p>Eventually, one day, we were walking past his house and his father came out. As a man, I see P's behaviour as the actions of of a socially awkward and immature boy and my retaliation, especially with the power dynamic, was way too far.</p><p><br></p><p>Eventually, one day, we were walking past his house and his father came out. I hadn't and was really confused until he admitted P said those things to him.  I confronted P and he denied it.  I also noticed one of my books, that P had been reading in my room, was gone.</p><p><br></p><p>Another friend, who was at P's house said he'd seen it there and P was showing it to him. I was furious and felt betrayed.  I demanded the book back and he denied it. The other friend stole it back for me (it was mine and had my name in it) when he visited P's house.</p><p><br></p><p>Here's where I feel I went OTT. Instead of just cutting him out of the group, I began to bully him. Mostly verbally, mocking him but also even making silent calls to his house.  This went on for four or 5 weeks and included my friend group. I think this went too far.  I was angry and upset at the time and felt he needed to be punished.  As a man, I see P's behaviour as the actions of of a socially awkward and immature boy and my retaliation, especially with the power dynamic, was way too far.</p><p><br></p><p>Eventually, one day, we were walking past his house and his father came out. Furious at the way we were treating P and almost begging us to stop. I hadn't realised the effect we were having on P or his family.  After that, the behaviour stopped and I just left him alone. None of us wanted him back in our friend group, but I think we were all a little ashamed of our behaviour.</p><p><br></p><p>Years later, I saw him as an adult and apologised for the way I had treated him. It made me see myself in a light I didn't much like and even now, it fills me with shame. This went on for four or 5 weeks and included my friend group. I think this went too far.  I was angry and upset at the time and felt he needed to be punished.  As a man, I see P's behaviour as the actions of of a socially awkward and immature boy and my retaliation, especially with the power dynamic, was way too far.</p><p><br></p><p>Eventually, one day, we were walking past his house and his father came out. This went on for four or 5 weeks and included my friend group. I think this went too far.  I was angry and upset at the time and felt he needed to be punished.  As a man, I see P's behaviour as the actions of of a socially awkward and immature boy and my retaliation, especially with the power dynamic, was way too far.</p><p><br></p><p>Eventually, one day, we were walking past his house and his father came out. As a man, I see P's behaviour as the actions of of a socially awkward and immature boy and my retaliation, especially with the power dynamic, was way too far.</p><p><br></p><p>Eventually, one day, we were walking past his house and his father came out. Furious at the way we were treating P and almost begging us to stop. I hadn't realised the effect we were having on P or his family.  After that, the behaviour stopped and I just left him alone. None of us wanted him back in our friend group, but I think we were all a little ashamed of our behaviour.</p><p><br></p><p>Years later, I saw him as an adult and apologised for the way I had treated him. It made me see myself in a light I didn't much like and even now, it fills me with shame. The other friend stole it back for me (it was mine and had my name in it) when he visited P's house.</p><p><br></p><p>Here's where I feel I went OTT. This went on for four or 5 weeks and included my friend group. I think this went too far.  I was angry and upset at the time and felt he needed to be punished.  As a man, I see P's behaviour as the actions of of a socially awkward and immature boy and my retaliation, especially with the power dynamic, was way too far.</p><p><br></p><p>Eventually, one day, we were walking past his house and his father came out. None of us wanted him back in our friend group, but I think we were all a little ashamed of our behaviour.</p><p><br></p><p>Years later, I saw him as an adult and apologised for the way I had treated him. I later noticed that one of my closest friends was acting colder towards me, so I confronted him about it.  He said he was angry because of all the bad things I had said about him.  I hadn't and was really confused until he admitted P said those things to him.  I confronted P and he denied it.  I also noticed one of my books, that P had been reading in my room, was gone.</p><p><br></p><p>Another friend, who was at P's house said he'd seen it there and P was showing it to him. I was furious and felt betrayed.  I demanded the book back and he denied it. The other friend stole it back for me (it was mine and had my name in it) when he visited P's house.</p><p><br></p><p>Here's where I feel I went OTT. Instead of just cutting him out of the group, I began to bully him. Mostly verbally, mocking him but also even making silent calls to his house.  This went on for four or 5 weeks and included my friend group. I think this went too far.  I was angry and upset at the time and felt he needed to be punished.  As a man, I see P's behaviour as the actions of of a socially awkward and immature boy and my retaliation, especially with the power dynamic, was way too far.</p><p><br></p><p>Eventually, one day, we were walking past his house and his father came out. Furious at the way we were treating P and almost begging us to stop. I hadn't realised the effect we were having on P or his family.  After that, the behaviour stopped and I just left him alone. None of us wanted him back in our friend group, but I think we were all a little ashamed of our behaviour.</p><p><br></p><p>Years later, I saw him as an adult and apologised for the way I had treated him. It made me see myself in a light I didn't much like and even now, it fills me with shame.  I sometimes read stories of bullying and once upon a time, I would have identified myself with the victim (as I had been bullied before at school, as had a lot of people, in fact it was one of the main reasons I befriended P in the first place.)</p><p><br></p><p>I realised I was motivated by the feeling of ingratitude and betrayal by someone I had tried to help for altruistic reasons, passing the hurt onto him, however I definitely believe it was morally wrong and that P was hurt by it and that my subsequent actions cancelled</p>"},{"timestamp":"15:11:57","text":"<p>When I was about 12, I had befriended a lonely boy (P) in our class who had recently joined and had no friends. I had included him in our friendship group, invited him out with my friends etc and had him over to my house. </p><p>We became friends and P was a regular part of the group.  After a while, he sometimes hung out with some of my friends without me, which was cool.  P sometimes said things about my friends that I didn't like. I defended them and he stopped. I later noticed that one of my closest friends was acting colder towards me, so I confronted him about it.  He said he was angry because of all the bad things I had said about him.  I hadn't and was really confused until he admitted P said those things to him.  I confronted P and he denied it.  I also noticed one of my books, that P had been reading in my room, was gone.</p><p><br></p><p>Another friend, who was at P's house said he'd seen it there and P was showing it to him. I was furious and felt betrayed.  I demanded the book back and he denied it. The other friend stole it back for me (it was mine and had my name in it) when he visited P's house.</p><p><br></p><p>Here's where I feel I went OTT. Instead of just cutting him out of the group, I began to bully him. Mostly verbally, mocking him but also even making silent calls to his house.  This went on for four or 5 weeks and included my friend group. I think this went too far.  I was angry and upset at the time and felt he needed to be punished.  As a man, I see P's behaviour as the actions of of a socially awkward and immature boy and my retaliation, especially with the power dynamic, was way too far.</p><p><br></p><p>Eventually, one day, we were walking past his house and his father came out. Furious at the way we were treating P and almost begging us to stop. I hadn't realised the effect we were having on P or his family.  After that, the behaviour stopped and I just left him alone. None of us wanted him back in our friend group, but I think we were all a little ashamed of our behaviour.</p><p><br></p><p>Years later, I saw him as an adult and apologised for the way I had treated him. It made me see myself in a light I didn't much like and even now, it fills me with shame.  I sometimes read stories of bullying and once upon a time, I would have identified myself with the victim (as I had been bullied before at school, as had a lot of people, in fact it was one of the main reasons I befriended P in the first place.)</p><p><br></p><p>I realised I was motivated by the feeling of ingratitude and betrayal by someone I had tried to help for altruistic reasons, passing the hurt onto him, however I definitely believe it was morally wrong and that P was hurt by it and that my subsequent actions cancelled out any moral good I had originally intended.</p>"}]}
